  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1158445-1199230.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-116-20140325.html "Link to previous version of section 116"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scope of developments

- **[[117]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-117
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every environmental assessment of a
  proposal for a development shall include a determination by the Review
  Board of the scope of the development, subject to any guidelines made
  under section 120.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors to be considered

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every environmental assessment and environmental
  impact review of a proposal for a development shall include a
  consideration of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the impact of the development on the environment,
    including the impact of malfunctions or accidents that may occur in
    connection with the development and any cumulative impact that is
    likely to result from the development in combination with other
    developments;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the significance of any such impact;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any comments submitted by members of the public in
    accordance with the regulations or the rules of practice and
    procedure of the Review Board;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} where the development is likely to have a
    significant adverse impact on the environment, the need for
    mitigative or remedial measures; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any other matter, such as the need for the
    development and any available alternatives to it, that the Review
    Board or any responsible minister, after consulting the Review
    Board, determines to be relevant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional factors

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An environmental impact review of a proposal for a
  development shall also include a consideration of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the purpose of the development;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} alternative means, if any, of carrying out the
    development that are technically and economically feasible, and the
    impact on the environment of such alternative means;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the need for any follow-up program and the
    requirements of such a program; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the capacity of any renewable resources that are
    likely to be significantly affected by the development to meet
    existing and future needs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Joint panels

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (2) and (3) apply in respect of an
  examination of a proposal for a development by a review panel, or a
  joint panel, established jointly by the Review Board and any other
  person or body.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 25, s. 117
- 2005, c. 1, s. 70
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-117-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 117"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issuance of licence, permit or other
authorization

- **[[118]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-118 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  licence, permit or other authorization required for the carrying out
  of a development shall not be issued under any federal, territorial,
  Tlicho or Déline law unless the requirements of this Part have been
  complied with in relation to the development.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Gwich'in or Sahtu First Nation, the Tlicho
  Government, the Déline Got'ine Government, a local government or a
  department or agency of the federal or territorial government proposes
  to carry out a development that does not require a licence, permit or
  other authorization under any federal, territorial, Tlicho or Déline
  law, it shall comply with the requirements of this Part before taking
  any irrevocable action in relation to the development.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 25, s. 118
- 2005, c. 1, s. 71
- 2015, c. 24, s. 38
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-118-20050804.html "Link to previous version of section 118"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emergencies excluded

**[[119]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-119 .sectionLabel}** No preliminary
screening, environmental assessment or environmental impact review is
required to be conducted in relation to a proposal for a development

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} that is carried out in response to a national
  emergency for which special temporary measures are being taken under
  the [Emergencies Act](/eng/acts/E-4.5); or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} that is carried out in response to an emergency in
  circumstances such that it is in the interest of protecting property
  or the environment or in the interest of public welfare, health or
  safety to carry out the proposal forthwith.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Guidelines

**[[120]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-120 .sectionLabel}** Following consultation
with first nations, the Tlicho Government and the federal and
territorial Ministers and subject to any regulations made under
paragraph 143(1)(a), the Review Board may establish guidelines
respecting the process established by this Part, including guidelines

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} for the determination of the scope of developments by
  the Review Board;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} for the form and content of reports made under this
  Part; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} for the submission and distribution of environmental
  impact statements and for public notification of such submission
  pursuant to paragraphs 134(1)(b) and (c).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 25, s. 120
- 2005, c. 1, s. 72
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-120-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 120"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Written reasons

**[[121]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-121 .sectionLabel}** The Review Board, a
review panel of the Review Board, a review panel or a joint panel
established jointly by the Review Board and any other person or body,
the federal Minister, a responsible minister, a designated regulatory
agency, a regulatory authority, a department or agency of the federal or
territorial government, a local government, the Gwich'in or Sahtu First
Nation, the Tlicho Government or the Déline Got'ine Government shall
issue and make available to the public written reasons for any decision
or recommendation made under the process established by this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 25, s. 121
- 2005, c. 1, s. 73
- 2015, c. 24, s. 39
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-121-20050804.html "Link to previous version of section 121"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delegation by federal Minister

**[[122]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-122 .sectionLabel}** The federal Minister
may, in relation to a proposed development, delegate to any responsible
minister the federal Minister's duty to distribute reports made under
this Part, to participate in decisions made following the consideration
of such reports and to distribute decisions so made.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exercise of powers under other Acts

**[[123]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-123 .sectionLabel}** For greater certainty,
the Review Board may exercise any function conferred on it by or under
any Act of Parliament or delegated to it under any Act of Parliament.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation

**[[123.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-123.1 .sectionLabel}** In conducting a
review or examination of the impact on the environment of a development,
a review panel of the Review Board or a review panel, or a joint panel,
established jointly by the Review Board and any other person or body,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall carry out any consultations that are required
  by any of the land claim agreements; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} may carry out other consultations with any persons
  who use an area where the development might have an impact on the
  environment.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2005, c. 1, s. 74
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conflict of interest

- **[[123.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-123.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person shall not be appointed, or
  continue, as a member of a review panel of the Review Board or of a
  review panel, or a joint panel, established jointly by the Review
  Board and any other person or body, if doing so would place the member
  in a material conflict of interest.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Status or entitlements under agreement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person is not placed in a material conflict of
  interest merely because of any status or entitlement conferred on the
  person under the Gwich'in Agreement, the Sahtu Agreement, the Tlicho
  Agreement or any other agreement between a first nation and Her
  Majesty in right of Canada for the settlement of a claim to lands or
  under the Déline Agreement.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2005, c. 1, s. 74
- 2015, c. 24, s. 40
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-123.2-20050804.html "Link to previous version of section 123.2"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Nominations

**[[123.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-123.3 .sectionLabel}** In appointing a
review panel of the Review Board or a review panel, or a joint panel,
established jointly by the Review Board and any other person or body,
the Review Board and those other persons or bodies shall comply with the
requirements of land claim agreements respecting nominations for
appointment.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2005, c. 1, s. 74
:::

### [Preliminary Screening]{.HTitleText2} {#h-347475 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to regulator

- **[[124]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-124
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where, pursuant to any federal or
  territorial law specified in the regulations made under paragraph
  143(1)(b), an application is made to a regulatory authority or
  designated regulatory agency for a licence, permit or other
  authorization required for the carrying out of a development, the
  authority or agency shall notify the Review Board in writing of the
  application and conduct a preliminary screening of the proposal for
  the development, unless the development is exempted from preliminary
  screening because

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} its impact on the environment is declared to be
    insignificant by regulations made under paragraph 143(1)(c); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an examination of the proposal is declared to be
    inappropriate for reasons of national security by those regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proposal not requiring application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a development that does not require a licence,
  permit or other authorization under any federal or territorial law is
  proposed to be carried out by a department or agency of the federal or
  territorial government or by the Gwich'in or Sahtu First Nation or the
  Tlicho Government, the body proposing to carry out that development
  shall, after notifying the Review Board in writing of the proposal for
  the development, conduct a preliminary screening of the proposal,
  unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in its opinion, the impact of the development on
    the environment will be manifestly insignificant; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the development is exempted from preliminary
    screening for a reason referred to in paragraph (1)(a) or (b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Preliminary screening by Gwich'in,
  Sahtu or Tlicho

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Gwich'in First Nation, the Sahtu First Nation or
  the Tlicho Government, as the case may be, may conduct a preliminary
  screening of a proposal for a development to determine whether to
  refer the proposal for an environmental assessment in accordance with
  paragraph 126(2)(b) or (c).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cooperation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where more than one body conducts a preliminary
  screening in respect of a development, any of them may consult the
  others, adopt another's report or participate in a joint preliminary
  screening and, where one of them is a board established under Part 3
  or 4, the others are not required to conduct a preliminary screening.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 25, s. 124
- 2005, c. 1, s. 75
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-124-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 124"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Outside local government territory

- **[[125]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-125
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Except as provided by subsection
  (2), a body that conducts a preliminary screening of a proposal shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} determine and report to the Review Board whether,
    in its opinion, the development might have a significant adverse
    impact on the environment or might be a cause of public concern; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where it so determines in the affirmative, refer
    the proposal to the Review Board for an environmental assessment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delay

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} If the body determines under subsection (1) that
  the development will not have a significant adverse impact on the
  environment and will not be a cause of public concern,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a regulatory authority, a designated regulatory
    agency or the Tlicho Government shall not issue a licence, permit or
    other authorization for the development before the end of 10 days
    after the day on which the Review Board receives the report of the
    determination; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if no licence, permit or other authorization is
    required under any federal, territorial or Tlicho law for the
    development, the person or body that proposes to carry it out shall
    not proceed before the end of 10 days after the day on which the
    Review Board receives the report of the determination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Boards established under Part 3 or 4

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} If a board established under Part 3 or 4 is the
  body that determines under subsection (1) that the development will
  not have a significant adverse impact on the environment and will not
  be a cause of public concern, that board may issue a licence, permit
  or other authorization for the development before the end of 10 days
  after the day on which the Review Board receives the report of the
  determination but that licence, permit or other authorization shall
  come into force only after the end of that 10-day period and if no
  referral under subsection 126(2) or (3) has been made during that
  period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Computation of time

  [(1.3)]{.lawlabel} If more than one body conducts a preliminary
  screening in respect of a development and they each determine that the
  development will not have a significant adverse impact on the
  environment and will not be a cause of public concern, the 10-day
  period referred to in subsections (1.1) and (1.2) begins after the day
  on which the Review Board receives the last of their reports of
  determination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Within local government territory

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a proposed development is wholly within the
  boundaries of a local government, a body that conducts a preliminary
  screening of the proposal shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} determine and report to the Review Board whether,
    in its opinion, the development is likely to have a significant
    adverse impact on air, water or renewable resources or might be a
    cause of public concern; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where it so determines in the affirmative, refer
    the proposal to the Review Board for an environmental assessment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delay

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a body determines under subsection (2) that the
  development is unlikely to have a significant adverse impact on air,
  water or renewable resources and will not be a cause of public
  concern,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a regulatory authority, a designated regulatory
    agency or the Tlicho Government shall not issue a licence, permit or
    other authorization for the development before the end of 10 days
    after the day on which the Review Board receives the report of the
    determination; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if no licence, permit or other authorization is
    required under any federal, territorial or Tlicho law for the
    development, the person or body that proposes to carry it out shall
    not proceed before the end of 10 days after the day on which the
    Review Board receives the report of the determination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Boards established under Part 3 or 4

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a board established under Part 3 or 4 is the body
  that determines under subsection (2) that the development is unlikely
  to have a significant adverse impact on air, water or renewable
  resources and will not be a cause of public concern, that board may
  issue a licence, permit or other authorization for the development
  before the end of 10 days after the day on which the Review Board
  receives the report of the determination but that licence, permit or
  other authorization shall come into force only after the end of that
  10-day period and if no referral under subsection 126(2) or (3) has
  been made during that period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Computation of time

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If more than one body conducts a preliminary
  screening in respect of a development and they each determine that the
  development is unlikely to have a significant adverse impact on air,
  water or renewable resources and will not be a cause of public
  concern, the 10-day period referred to in subsections (3) and (4)
  begins after the day on which the Review Board receives the last of
  their reports of determination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy of report

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A body that conducts a preliminary screening of a
  proposal shall provide a copy of its report to the person or body that
  proposes to carry out the development.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 25, s. 125
- [2019, c. 19, s. 17](#1151760-1173450){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1151760-1173450.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-125-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 125"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Environmental Assessment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-347500 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Referral on preliminary screening

- **[[126]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-126 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Review Board shall conduct an environmental assessment of a proposal
  for a development that is referred to the Review Board following a
  preliminary screening pursuant to section 125.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Referral from department, agency, first
  nation or local government

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding any determination on a preliminary
  screening, the Review Board shall conduct an environmental assessment
  of a proposal for a development that is referred to it by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a regulatory authority, designated regulatory
    agency or department or agency of the federal or territorial
    government;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Gwich'in or Sahtu First Nation, in the case of
    a development to be carried out in its settlement area or a
    development that might have an impact on the environment in that
    settlement area;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the Tlicho Government, in the case of a development
    to be carried out wholly or partly in the part of Monfwi Gogha De
    Niitlee that is in the Northwest Territories or a development that
    might have an impact on the environment in that part; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a local government, in the case of a development to
    be carried out within its boundaries or a development that might
    have an impact on the environment within its boundaries.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review Board's own motion

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding any determination on a preliminary
  screening, the Review Board may conduct an environmental assessment of
  a proposal for a development on its own motion.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}For greater certainty

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, subsections (2) and (3) apply
  even if a preliminary screening has not been commenced or, if
  commenced, has not been completed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Review Board shall give notice of a referral of a
  proposal under subsection (2), or of its decision to conduct an
  environmental assessment under subsection (3), to the person or body
  that proposes to carry out the development.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 25, s. 126
- 2005, c. 1, s. 76
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-126-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 126"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of *EARP Order* and *CEAA*

**[[127]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-127 .sectionLabel}** In an environmental
assessment of a proposal for a development, the Review Board shall take
into account any report made in relation to that proposal before the
coming into force of this Part pursuant to the Environmental Assessment
and Review Process Guidelines Order, approved by Order in Council P.C.
1984-2132 of June 21, 1984, and registered as SOR/84-467, or pursuant to
the [Canadian Environmental Assessment Act](/eng/acts/C-15.2).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation

**[[127.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-127.1 .sectionLabel}** Before completing
an environmental assessment of a proposal for a development that is to
be carried out wholly or partly on first nation lands as defined in
section 51 or on Tlicho lands, the Review Board shall consult the first
nation on whose lands the development is to be carried out or, if the
development is to be carried out on Tlicho lands, the Tlicho Government.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2005, c. 1, s. 77
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assessment by Review Board

- **[[128]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-128 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  completing an environmental assessment of a proposal for a
  development, the Review Board shall,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the development is not likely in its opinion
    to have any significant adverse impact on the environment or to be a
    cause of significant public concern, determine that an environmental
    impact review of the proposal need not be conducted;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the development is likely in its opinion to
    have a significant adverse impact on the environment,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} order that an environmental impact review of the
      proposal be conducted, subject to paragraph 130(1)(c), or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} recommend that the approval of the proposal be
      made subject to the imposition of such measures as it considers
      necessary to prevent the significant adverse impact;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} where the development is likely in its opinion to
    be a cause of significant public concern, order that an
    environmental impact review of the proposal be conducted, subject to
    paragraph 130(1)(c); and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} where the development is likely in its opinion to
    cause an adverse impact on the environment so significant that it
    cannot be justified, recommend that the proposal be rejected without
    an environmental impact review.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review Board's report

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Review Board shall, within nine months after the
  day on which a proposal is referred to it under section 125 or
  subsection 126(2) or the day on which it starts to conduct an
  assessment under subsection 126(3), complete its environmental
  assessment and make a report of that assessment to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the federal Minister, who shall distribute it to
    every responsible minister;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any designated regulatory agency from which a
    licence, permit or other authorization is required for the carrying
    out of the development; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the development is to be carried out wholly or
    partly on Tlicho lands, the Tlicho Government.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit --- hearings

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} If the Review Board holds a public hearing during
  the conduct of the environmental assessment, the time limit referred
  to in subsection (2) is extended to 16 months.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by federal
  Minister

  [(2.2)]{.lawlabel} The federal Minister may, at the request of the
  Review Board, extend the time limit referred to in subsection (2) or
  (2.1) by a maximum of two months to take into account circumstances
  that are specific to the proposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by Governor in
  Council

  [(2.3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the recommendation
  of the federal Minister, further extend the time limit extended under
  subsection (2.2) any number of times.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Excluded period

  [(2.4)]{.lawlabel} If the Review Board requires the person or body
  that proposes to carry out the development to provide information, or
  collect information or undertake a study with respect to the
  development, then the period that is taken by that person or body, in
  the Review Board's opinion, to comply with the requirement is not
  included in the calculation of the time limit or of its extension.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copies of report

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Review Board shall provide a copy of its report
  to any body that conducted a preliminary screening of the proposal, to
  any body that referred the proposal to the Review Board under
  subsection 126(2) and to the person or body that proposes to carry out
  the development.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Areas identified

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Review Board shall identify in its report any
  area within or outside the Mackenzie Valley in which the development
  is likely, in its opinion, to have a significant adverse impact or to
  be a cause of significant public concern and specify the extent to
  which that area is affected.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 25, s. 128
- 2005, c. 1, s. 78
- 2014, c. 2, s. 206
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-128-20050804.html "Link to previous version of section 128"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delay

**[[129]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-129 .sectionLabel}** Where the Review Board
makes a determination under paragraph 128(1)(a),

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a regulatory authority, a designated regulatory
  agency or the Tlicho Government shall not issue a licence, permit or
  other authorization for the development, and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} where no licence, permit or authorization is required
  under any federal, territorial or Tlicho law for the development, the
  person or body that proposes to carry it out shall not proceed,

before the expiration of ten days after receiving the report of the
Review Board.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 25, s. 129
- 2005, c. 1, s. 79
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-129-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 129"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision by ministers

- **[[130]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-130
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} After considering the report of an
  environmental assessment, the federal Minister and the responsible
  ministers to whom the report was distributed may agree

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to order an environmental impact review of a
    proposal, notwithstanding a determination under paragraph 128(1)(a);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where a recommendation is made under subparagraph
    128(1)(b)(ii) or paragraph 128(1)(d),

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to adopt the recommendation or refer it back to
      the Review Board for further consideration, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} after consulting the Review Board, to adopt the
      recommendation with modifications or reject it and order an
      environmental impact review of the proposal; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} irrespective of the determination in the report, to
    refer the proposal to the Minister of the Environment, following
    consultation with that Minister, for the purpose of a joint review
    under the [Impact Assessment Act](/eng/acts/I-2.75), if the federal
    Minister and the responsible ministers determine that it is in the
    national interest to do so.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} Before making an order under paragraph (1)(a) or a
  referral under paragraph (1)(c), the federal Minister and the
  responsible ministers shall consult

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Gwich'in First Nation, if the development is to
    be carried out wholly or partly on its first nation lands, as
    defined in section 51;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Sahtu First Nation, if the development is to be
    carried out wholly or partly on its first nation lands, as defined
    in section 51; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the Tlicho Government, if the development is to be
    carried out wholly or partly on Tlicho lands.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Areas identified

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an environmental impact review of a proposal is
  ordered under subsection (1), the federal Minister and responsible
  ministers shall identify any area within or outside the Mackenzie
  Valley in which the development is likely, in their opinion, to have a
  significant adverse impact or to be a cause of significant public
  concern and specify the extent to which that area is affected.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional information

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the federal Minister and responsible ministers
  consider any new information that was not before the Review Board, or
  any matter of public concern not referred to in the Review Board's
  reasons, the new information or matter shall be identified in the
  decision made under this section and in any consultation under
  paragraph (1)(b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Distribution of decision

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The federal Minister shall distribute a decision made
  under this section to the Review Board and to every first nation,
  local government, regulatory authority and department and agency of
  the federal or territorial government affected by the decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limits

  [(4.01)]{.lawlabel} The federal Minister shall distribute a decision
  made under this section within three months after the day on which the
  federal Minister receives the Review Board's report of an
  environmental assessment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit --- hearings

  [(4.02)]{.lawlabel} If the Review Board holds a public hearing during
  the conduct of the environmental assessment, the time limit referred
  to in subsection (4.01) is extended to five months.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by federal
  Minister

  [(4.03)]{.lawlabel} The federal Minister may extend the time limit
  referred to in subsection (4.01) or (4.02) by a maximum of two months
  to take into account circumstances that are specific to the proposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by Governor in
  Council

  [(4.04)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the recommendation
  of the federal Minister, further extend the time limit extended under
  subsection (4.03) any number of times.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit --- further consideration

  [(4.05)]{.lawlabel} If a recommendation is referred back to the Review
  Board for further consideration under subparagraph (1)(b)(i), the time
  taken for that referral and consideration is included in the
  calculation of the time limit set out in subsection (4.01) or (4.02)
  or of its extension.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Excluded period

  [(4.06)]{.lawlabel} If the federal Minister or the Review Board
  requires the person or body that proposes to carry out the development
  to provide information, or collect information or undertake a study
  with respect to the development, then the period that is taken by that
  person or body, in the federal Minister's or the Review Board's
  opinion, as the case may be, to comply with the requirement is not
  included in the calculation of the time limit under subsection (4.01)
  or (4.02) or of its extension.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification --- paragraph (1)(c)

  [(4.07)]{.lawlabel} If, following an order by the Review Board that an
  environmental impact review of a proposal be conducted, the federal
  Minister and the responsible ministers do not refer a proposal to the
  Minister of the Environment under paragraph (1)(c), the federal
  Minister shall so advise the Review Board in writing within three
  months after the day on which the federal Minister receives the Review
  Board's report of an environmental assessment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit --- hearings

  [(4.08)]{.lawlabel} If the Review Board holds a public hearing during
  the conduct of the environmental assessment, the time limit referred
  to in subsection (4.07) is extended to five months.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by federal
  Minister

  [(4.09)]{.lawlabel} The federal Minister may extend the time limit
  referred to in subsection (4.07) or (4.08) by a maximum of two months
  to take into account circumstances that are specific to the proposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by Governor in
  Council

  [(4.1)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the recommendation
  of the federal Minister, further extend the time limit extended under
  subsection (4.09) any number of times.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of decision

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The federal Minister and responsible ministers shall
  carry out a decision made under this section to the extent of their
  respective authorities. A first nation, local government, regulatory
  authority or department or agency of the federal or territorial
  government affected by a decision made under this section shall act in
  conformity with the decision to the extent of their respective
  authorities.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 25, s. 130
- 2005, c. 1, s. 80
- 2014, c. 2, s. 208
- [2019, c. 28, s. 188](#1158445-1199232){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1158445-1199232.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-130-20140325.html "Link to previous version of section 130"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision by designated agency

- **[[131]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-131 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  designated regulatory agency shall, after considering a report of the
  Review Board containing a recommendation made under subparagraph
  128(1)(b)(ii) or paragraph 128(1)(d),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} adopt the recommendation or refer it back to the
    Review Board for further consideration; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} after consulting the Review Board, adopt the
    recommendation with modifications or reject it and order an
    environmental impact review of the proposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The designated regulatory agency shall make a
  decision under subsection (1) within three months after the day on
  which the agency receives the Review Board's report of an
  environmental assessment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit --- hearings

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} If the Review Board holds a public hearing during
  the conduct of the environmental assessment, the time limit referred
  to in subsection (1.1) is extended to five months.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by designated
  regulatory agency

  [(1.3)]{.lawlabel} The designated regulatory agency may extend the
  time limit referred to in subsection (1.1) or (1.2) by a maximum of
  two months to take into account circumstances that are specific to the
  proposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by Governor in
  Council

  [(1.4)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the recommendation
  of the Minister responsible for the designated regulatory agency,
  further extend the time limit extended under subsection (1.3) any
  number of times.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit --- further consideration

  [(1.5)]{.lawlabel} If a recommendation is referred back to the Review
  Board for further consideration under paragraph (1)(a), the time taken
  for that referral and consideration is included in the calculation of
  the time limit set out in subsection (1.1) or (1.2) or of its
  extension.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Excluded period

  [(1.6)]{.lawlabel} If the designated regulatory agency or the Review
  Board requires the person or body that proposes to carry out the
  development to provide information, or collect information or
  undertake a study with respect to the development, then the period
  that is taken by that person or body, in the designated regulatory
  agency's or the Review Board's opinion, as the case may be, to comply
  with the requirement is not included in the calculation of the time
  limit under subsection (1.1) or (1.2) or of its extension.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of decision

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A designated regulatory agency shall carry out, to
  the extent of its authority, any recommendation that it adopts.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Areas identified

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where an environmental impact review of a proposal is
  ordered under subsection (1), the designated regulatory agency shall
  identify any area within or outside the Mackenzie Valley in which the
  development is likely, in its opinion, to have a significant adverse
  impact or to be a cause of significant public concern and specify the
  extent to which that area is affected.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional information

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a designated regulatory agency considers any new
  information that was not before the Review Board, or any matter of
  public concern that was not referred to in the Review Board's reasons,
  the new information or matter shall be identified in the decision made
  under this section and in any consultation under paragraph (1)(b).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 25, s. 131
- 2014, c. 2, s. 209
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-131-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 131"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision by Tlicho Government

- **[[131.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-131.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a development is to be carried
  out wholly or partly on Tlicho lands, the Tlicho Government shall,
  after considering a report of the Review Board containing a
  recommendation made under subparagraph 128(1)(b)(ii),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} adopt the recommendation or refer it back to the
    Review Board for further consideration; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} after consulting the Review Board, adopt the
    recommendation with modifications or reject it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of decision

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Tlicho Government shall carry out, to the extent
  of its authority, any recommendation that it adopts.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional information

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the Tlicho Government considers any new
  information that was not before the Review Board, or any matter of
  public concern that was not referred to in the Review Board's reasons,
  the new information or matter shall be identified in the decision made
  under this section and in any consultation under paragraph (1)(b).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2005, c. 1, s. 81
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conservation

**[[131.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-131.2 .sectionLabel}** In making a
decision under paragraph 130(1)(b) or subsection 131(1) or 131.1(1), the
federal Minister and the responsible ministers, a designated regulatory
agency or the Tlicho Government, as the case may be, shall consider the
importance of the conservation of the lands, waters and wildlife of the
Mackenzie Valley on which the development might have an impact.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2005, c. 1, s. 81
:::

### [Environmental Impact Review]{.HTitleText2} {#h-347642 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of review panel

- **[[132]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-132
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 138 to 141, an
  environmental impact review of a proposal for a development shall be
  conducted by a review panel consisting of three or more members
  appointed by the Review Board, including a chairperson.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expert members

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A review panel may, in addition to members of the
  Review Board, include as members of the panel persons having
  particular expertise related to the development.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Board members to participate in
  appointments

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The members of the Review Board who participate in
  the appointment of a review panel must include in equal numbers

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} members who were appointed on the nomination of a
    first nation or the Tlicho Government; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} members not so appointed, other than the
    chairperson.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Review Board shall appoint members to a review
  panel within three months after the day on which the Review Board is
  advised, as the case may be,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} of an order for an environmental impact review
    under paragraph 130(1)(a), subparagraph 130(1)(b)(ii) or paragraph
    131(1)(b); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with subsection 130(4.07), that the
    proposal has not been referred to the Minister of the Environment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by federal
  Minister

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The federal Minister may, at the request of the
  Review Board, extend the time limit referred to in subsection (4) by a
  maximum of two months to take into account circumstances that are
  specific to the proposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by Governor in
  Council

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the recommendation of
  the federal Minister, further extend the time limit extended under
  subsection (5) any number of times.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Excluded period

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} If the Review Board requires the person or body that
  proposes to carry out the development to provide information, or
  collect information or undertake a study with respect to the
  development, then the period that is taken by that person or body, in
  the Board's opinion, to comply with the requirement is not included in
  the calculation of the time limit under subsection (4) or of its
  extension.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 25, s. 132
- 2005, c. 1, s. 82
- 2014, c. 2, s. 212
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-132-20050804.html "Link to previous version of section 132"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers and duties of panel

- **[[133]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-133 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  review panel may exercise the powers and shall perform the duties of
  the Review Board in the conduct of an environmental impact review.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Instructions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A review panel may issue, with respect to an impact
  statement referred to in paragraph 134(1)(b), special instructions not
  inconsistent with any guidelines issued under section 120.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Coordination

**[[133.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-133.1 .sectionLabel}** The Review Board
shall to the extent possible coordinate any environmental impact review
conducted by a review panel of a proposal for a development that, as
determined by the Review Board, is to be carried out partly outside the
Mackenzie Valley with any examination of the environmental impact of
that development conducted by another authority responsible for the
examination of environmental effects of the part of the development to
be carried out outside the Mackenzie Valley.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2005, c. 1, s. 83
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Components of review

- **[[134]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-134 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  environmental impact review of a proposal for a development includes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the preparation by the Review Board of terms of
    reference for the review panel, after consultation with the
    responsible ministers, with any first nation affected by the
    proposal and, if the Board has determined that the development is
    likely to have a significant adverse impact on the environment, or
    to be a cause of significant public concern, in Monfwi Gogha De
    Niitlee, with the Tlicho Government;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the submission of an impact statement by the
    applicant for a licence, permit or other authorization or such other
    person or body as proposes to carry out the development, and its
    distribution in accordance with any guidelines issued under section
    120 and any special instructions issued under subsection 133(2);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} public notification, in accordance with any such
    guidelines, of the submission of the impact statement;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} such analysis of the proposal as the review panel
    considers appropriate; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} public consultations or hearings in communities
    that will be affected by the development.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit --- terms of reference

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The Review Board shall fix the terms of reference
  for the review panel within three months after the day on which the
  Review Board is advised, as the case may be,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} of an order for an environmental impact review
    under paragraph 130(1)(a), subparagraph 130(1)(b)(ii) or paragraph
    131(1)(b); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with subsection 130(4.07), that the
    proposal has not been referred to the Minister of the Environment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by federal
  Minister

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} The federal Minister may, at the request of the
  Review Board, extend the time limit referred to in subsection (1.1) by
  a maximum of two months to take into account circumstances that are
  specific to the proposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by Governor in
  Council

  [(1.3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the recommendation
  of the federal Minister, further extend the time limit extended under
  subsection (1.2) any number of times.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Excluded period

  [(1.4)]{.lawlabel} If the Review Board requires the person or body
  that proposes to carry out the development to provide information, or
  collect information or undertake a study with respect to the
  development, then the period that is taken by that person or body, in
  the Board's opinion, to comply with the requirement is not included in
  the calculation of the time limit under subsection (1.1) or of its
  extension.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A review panel shall issue a report containing a
  summary of comments received from the public, an account of the
  panel's analysis, the conclusions of the panel and its recommendation
  whether the proposal for the development be approved, with or without
  mitigative or remedial measures or a follow-up program, or rejected.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review panel's report

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Within 15 months after the first day on which both
  the members of the panel are appointed and the terms of reference are
  established, the report of a review panel shall be submitted to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the federal Minister, who shall distribute it to
    every responsible minister;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any designated regulatory agency from which a
    licence, permit or other authorization is required for the carrying
    out of the development; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the Tlicho Government, if the development is to be
    carried out wholly or partly on Tlicho lands.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by federal
  Minister

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The federal Minister may, at the request of the
  review panel, extend the time limit referred to in subsection (3) by a
  maximum of two months to take into account circumstances that are
  specific to the proposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by Governor in
  Council

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the recommendation of
  the federal Minister, further extend the time limit extended under
  subsection (4) any number of times.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Excluded period

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If the review panel requires the person or body that
  proposes to carry out the development to provide information, or
  collect information or undertake a study with respect to the
  development, then the period that is taken by that person or body, in
  the review panel's opinion, to comply with the requirement is not
  included in the calculation of the time limit under subsection (3) or
  of its extension.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copy of report

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} A copy of the report of a review panel shall be
  provided to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Gwich'in First Nation, if the development is to
    be carried out wholly or partly on its first nation lands, as
    defined in section 51; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Sahtu First Nation, if the development is to be
    carried out wholly or partly on its first nation lands, as defined
    in section 51.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 25, s. 134
- 2005, c. 1, s. 84
- 2014, c. 2, s. 213
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-134-20140325.html "Link to previous version of section 134"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consideration of report by ministers

- **[[135]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-135
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} After considering the report of a
  review panel, the federal Minister and responsible ministers to whom
  the report was distributed may agree to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} adopt the recommendation of the review panel or
    refer it back to the panel for further consideration; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} after consulting the review panel, adopt the
    recommendation with modifications or reject it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the federal Minister and responsible ministers
  consider any new information that was not before the review panel, or
  any matter of public concern not referred to in the panel's reasons,
  the new information or the matter shall be identified in the decision
  made under this section and in their consultations under paragraph
  (1)(b).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Distribution of decision

- **[[136]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-136 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  federal Minister shall distribute a decision under section 135 to
  every first nation, local government, regulatory authority and
  department or agency of the territorial or federal government affected
  by the decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limits

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The federal Minister shall distribute the decision
  within six months after the day on which the federal Minister received
  the review panel's report.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by federal
  Minister

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} The federal Minister may extend the time limit
  referred to in subsection (1.1) by a maximum of two months to take
  into account circumstances that are specific to the proposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by Governor in
  Council

  [(1.3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the recommendation
  of the federal Minister, further extend the time limit extended under
  subsection (1.2) any number of times.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit --- further consideration

  [(1.4)]{.lawlabel} If a recommendation is referred back to the review
  panel for further consideration under paragraph 135(1)(a), the time
  taken for that referral and consideration is included in the
  calculation of the time limit set out in subsection (1.1) or of its
  extension.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Excluded period

  [(1.5)]{.lawlabel} If the federal Minister or the review panel
  requires the person or body that proposes to carry out the development
  to provide information, or collect information or undertake a study
  with respect to the development, then the period that is taken by that
  person or body, in the federal Minister's or the review panel's
  opinion, as the case may be, to comply with the requirement is not
  included in the calculation of the time limit under subsection (1.1)
  or of its extension.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of decision

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The federal Minister and responsible ministers shall
  carry out a decision made under section 135 to the extent of their
  respective authorities. A first nation, local government, regulatory
  authority or department or agency of the federal or territorial
  government affected by a decision under that section shall act in
  conformity with the decision to the extent of their respective
  authorities.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 25, s. 136
- 2005, c. 1, s. 85
- 2014, c. 2, s. 214
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-136-20050804.html "Link to previous version of section 136"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consideration of report by agencies

- **[[137]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-137 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  designated regulatory agency shall, after considering the report of a
  review panel,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} adopt the recommendation of the review panel or
    refer it back to the panel for further consideration; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} after consulting the review panel, adopt the
    recommendation with modifications or reject it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limits

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The designated regulatory agency shall make a
  decision under subsection (1) within six months after the day on which
  it receives the review panel's report.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by designated
  regulatory agency

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} The designated regulatory agency may extend the
  time limit referred to in subsection (1.1) by a maximum of two months
  to take into account circumstances that are specific to the proposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by Governor in
  Council

  [(1.3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the recommendation
  of the Minister responsible for the designated regulatory agency,
  further extend the time limit extended under subsection (1.2) any
  number of times.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit --- further consideration

  [(1.4)]{.lawlabel} If a recommendation is referred back to the review
  panel for further consideration under paragraph (1)(a), the time taken
  for that referral and consideration is included in the calculation of
  the time limit set out in subsection (1.1) or of its extension.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Excluded period

  [(1.5)]{.lawlabel} If the designated regulatory agency or the review
  panel requires the person or body that proposes to carry out the
  development to provide information, or collect information or
  undertake a study with respect to the development, then the period
  that is taken by that person or body, in the designated regulatory
  agency's or the review panel's opinion, as the case may be, to comply
  with the requirement is not included in the calculation of the time
  limit under subsection (1.1) or of its extension.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a designated regulatory agency considers any new
  information that was not before the review panel, or any matter of
  public concern that was not referred to in the panel's reasons, such
  new information or such matter shall be identified in the decision of
  the agency and in any consultation under paragraph (1)(b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of decision

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A designated regulatory agency shall carry out, to
  the extent of its authority, any recommendation that it adopts.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 25, s. 137
- 2014, c. 2, s. 215
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-137-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 137"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision by Tlicho Government

- **[[137.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-137.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a development is to be carried
  out wholly or partly on Tlicho lands, the Tlicho Government shall,
  after considering the report of a review panel,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} adopt the recommendation of the review panel or
    refer it back to the panel for further consideration; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} after consulting the review panel, adopt the
    recommendation with modifications or reject it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Tlicho Government considers any new
  information that was not before the review panel, or any matter of
  public concern that was not referred to in the review panel's reasons,
  the new information or matter shall be identified in the decision made
  under this section and in any consultation under paragraph (1)(b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of decision

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Tlicho Government shall carry out, to the extent
  of its authority, any recommendation that it adopts.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2005, c. 1, s. 86
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conservation

**[[137.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-137.2 .sectionLabel}** In making a
decision under subsection 135(1), 137(1) or 137.1(1), the federal
Minister and the responsible ministers, a designated regulatory agency
or the Tlicho Government, as the case may be, shall consider the
importance of the conservation of the lands, waters and wildlife of the
Mackenzie Valley on which the development might have an impact.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2005, c. 1, s. 86
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation

**[[137.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-137.3 .sectionLabel}** Before making a
decision under subsection 135(1), 137(1) or 137.1(1) in respect of a
proposal for a development that, as determined by the Review Board, is
to be carried out partly outside the Mackenzie Valley, the person or
body making the decision shall take into consideration any report in
respect of the proposal that is submitted by a review panel established
under the [Impact Assessment Act](/eng/acts/I-2.75) and shall consult
the Impact Assessment Agency of Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2005, c. 1, s. 86
- 2014, c. 2, s. 217
- [2019, c. 28, s. 165](#1158312-1199237){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1158312-1199237.html"
  role="button"}
:::

Version](section-137.3-20140325.html "Link to previous version of section 137.3"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Cooperation and Joint Reviews]{.HTitleText2} {#h-347774 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report by review panel --- national
interest referral

- **[[138]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-138
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Within 15 months after the day on
  which a review panel that is the subject of an agreement entered into
  under subsection (3) or 138.1(1) is established under subsection 40(2)
  of the [Impact Assessment Act](/eng/acts/I-2.75) in respect of a
  proposal for a development that was referred under paragraph 130(1)(c)
  of this Act, the review panel shall, in addition to satisfying the
  requirements of paragraph 51(1)(e) of that Act, submit the report of
  its recommendations to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the federal Minister, who shall distribute it to
    every responsible minister;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any designated regulatory agency from which a
    licence, permit or other authorization is required for the carrying
    out of the development; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the Tlicho Government, if the development is to be
    carried out wholly or partly on Tlicho lands.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by federal
  Minister

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The federal Minister may, at the request of the
  review panel, extend the time limit referred to in subsection (1) by a
  maximum of two months to take into account circumstances that are
  specific to the proposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by Governor in
  Council

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the recommendation
  of the federal Minister, further extend the time limit extended under
  subsection (1.1) any number of times.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Excluded period

  [(1.3)]{.lawlabel} If the review panel requires the person or body
  that proposes to carry out the development to provide information, or
  collect information or undertake a study with respect to the
  development, then the period that is taken by that person or body, in
  the review panel's opinion, to comply with the requirement is not
  included in the calculation of the time limit under subsection (1) or
  of its extension.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provisions applicable

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An examination by a review panel referred to in
  subsection (1) stands in lieu of an environmental impact review and
  paragraphs 134(1)(b), (d) and (e) and sections 135 to 137.2 apply,
  with such modifications as may be required, in respect of the
  examination, except that a recommendation of a panel may not be
  referred back to the panel for further consideration.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Referral --- paragraph 130(1)(c)

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a proposal for a development, other than a
  proposal to which section 138.1 applies, is referred to the Minister
  of the Environment under paragraph 130(1)(c), the Review Board shall,
  within three months after the day on which the proposal was referred
  to that Minister, enter into an agreement with him or her for the
  purpose of jointly establishing a review panel and prescribing the
  manner of its examination of the development's impact on the
  environment in accordance with subsection 40(2) of the [Impact
  Assessment Act](/eng/acts/I-2.75).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by federal
  Minister

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The federal Minister may, at the request of the
  Review Board, extend the time limit referred to in subsection (3) by a
  maximum of two months to take into account circumstances that are
  specific to the proposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by Governor in
  Council

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the recommendation of
  the federal Minister, further extend the time limit extended under
  subsection (4) any number of times.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Excluded period

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If the Review Board requires the person or body that
  proposes to carry out the development to provide information, or
  collect information or undertake a study with respect to the
  development, then the period that is taken by that person or body, in
  the Review Board's opinion, to comply with the requirement is not
  included in the calculation of the time limit under subsection (3) or
  of its extension.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 25, s. 138
- 2005, c. 1, s. 87
- 2014, c. 2, s. 219
- [2019, c. 28, s. 166](#1158316-1199241){.wb-lbx

  role="button"}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agreement --- national interest referral

- **[[138.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-138.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a proposal for a development
  that, as determined by the Review Board, is to be carried out partly
  outside the Mackenzie Valley and either is to be carried out partly in
  Wekeezhii or might have an impact on the environment in Wekeezhii is
  referred to the Minister of the Environment under paragraph 130(1)(c),
  then the Review Board shall enter into an agreement with the Minister
  of the Environment for the purpose of jointly establishing a review
  panel and prescribing the manner of its examination of the
  development's impact on the environment in accordance with subsection
  40(2) of the [Impact Assessment Act](/eng/acts/I-2.75).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mediation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Review Board and the Minister of the
  Environment have not entered into an agreement under subsection (1)
  within the period fixed by any regulations, they shall participate in
  mediation in accordance with those regulations for the purpose of
  reaching an agreement under subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Arbitration

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the Review Board and the Minister of the
  Environment have not entered into an agreement under subsection (1) by
  the end of any mediation required under subsection (2), they may,
  within the period fixed by any regulations, by mutual agreement refer
  any unresolved matter to arbitration in accordance with those
  regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where no agreement

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsections (1) to (3), if, within the period
  fixed by the regulations, an agreement has not been entered into under
  this section, a panel of the Review Board shall conduct an
  environmental impact review of the development, but the review shall
  be limited to the part of the development to be carried out in the
  Mackenzie Valley.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2005, c. 1, s. 87
- 2014, c. 2, s. 220
- [2019, c. 28, s. 167](#1158325-1199243){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1158325-1199243.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Previous
Version](section-138.1-20140325.html "Link to previous version of section 138.1"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[139]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-139 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2014, c.
2, s. 221\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-139-20050804.html "Link to previous version of section 139"){.PITLink}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transboundary effects

- **[[140]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-140
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where it appears to the Review
  Board, during the environmental assessment of a development proposed
  to be carried out wholly within the Mackenzie Valley, that the
  development might have a significant adverse impact on the environment
  in a region outside the Mackenzie Valley, the Review Board shall so
  advise the authority responsible for the examination of environmental
  effects in that region and request its cooperation in the conduct of
  the assessment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agreement --- other authority

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the Review Board has determined that a
  development referred to in subsection (1), other than a development
  that has been referred to the Minister of the Environment under
  paragraph 130(1)(c), is likely to have a significant adverse impact on
  the environment in a region outside the Mackenzie Valley, the Review
  Board may, with the approval of the federal Minister, enter into an
  agreement with the authority responsible for the examination of
  environmental effects in that region to provide for

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the coordination of the respective examinations of
    the environmental impact of the development; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the examination of the environmental impact of the
    development by a joint panel established for that purpose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} Any agreement under paragraph (2)(b) shall be
  entered into within three months after the day on which the Review
  Board is advised, as the case may be,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} of an order for an environmental impact review
    under subparagraph 130(1)(b)(ii) or paragraph 131(1)(b); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with subsection 130(4.07), that the
    proposal has not been referred to the Minister of the Environment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by federal
  Minister

  [(2.2)]{.lawlabel} The federal Minister may, at the request of the
  Review Board, extend the time limit referred to in subsection (2.1) by
  a maximum of two months to take into account circumstances that are
  specific to the proposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by Governor in
  Council

  [(2.3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the recommendation
  of the federal Minister, further extend the time limit extended under
  subsection (2.2) any number of times.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Excluded period

  [(2.4)]{.lawlabel} If the Review Board requires the person or body
  that proposes to carry out the development to provide information, or
  collect information or undertake a study with respect to the
  development, then the period that is taken by that person or body, in
  the Review Board's opinion, to comply with the requirement is not
  included in the calculation of the time limit under subsection (2.1)
  or of its extension.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Environmental impact review

  [(2.5)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsections (2.1) to (2.4), if, within the
  time limit set out in those subsections, an agreement has not been
  entered into under paragraph (2)(b), a panel of the Review Board shall
  conduct an environmental impact review of the development.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Joint panel's report

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Within 15 months after the day on which a joint panel
  is established under paragraph (2)(b), the panel shall make a report
  of its recommendations to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the federal Minister, who shall distribute it to
    every responsible minister;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any designated regulatory agency from which a
    licence, permit or other authorization is required for the carrying
    out of the development; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the Tlicho Government, if the development is to be
    carried out wholly or partly on Tlicho lands.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by federal
  Minister

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} The federal Minister may, at the request of the
  joint panel, extend the time limit referred to in subsection (3) by a
  maximum of two months to take into account circumstances that are
  specific to the proposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by Governor in
  Council

  [(3.2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the recommendation
  of the federal Minister, further extend the time limit extended under
  subsection (3.1) any number of times.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Excluded period

  [(3.3)]{.lawlabel} If the joint panel requires the person or body that
  proposes to carry out the development to provide information, or
  collect information or undertake a study with respect to the
  development, then the period that is taken by that person or body, in
  the joint panel's opinion, to comply with the requirement is not
  included in the calculation of the time limit under subsection (3) or
  of its extension.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provisions applicable

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An examination by a joint panel established under
  subsection (2) stands in lieu of an environmental impact review of the
  proposal and paragraphs 134(1)(b), (d) and (e) and sections 135 to
  137.2 apply, with such modifications as may be required, in respect of
  the examination, except that a recommendation of a panel may not be
  referred back to the panel for further consideration.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1998, c. 25, s. 140
- 2005, c. 1, s. 88
- 2014, c. 2, s. 222
:::

[Previous
Version](section-140-20050804.html "Link to previous version of section 140"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Transregional and External Developments]{.HTitleText2} {#h-347845 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Environmental assessment

- **[[141]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-141 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  relation to a development that is proposed to be carried out partly in
  the Mackenzie Valley and partly in a region of the Northwest
  Territories, Yukon or Nunavut adjacent to the Mackenzie Valley, or
  partly in a province, as the case may be, the Review Board shall to
  the extent possible coordinate its environmental assessment functions
  with the functions of any authority responsible for the examination of
  environmental effects of the development in that region or province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agreement --- cases other than
  Wekeezhii

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If an environmental impact review is ordered under
  subparagraph 128(1)(b)(i), paragraph 128(1)(c) or 130(1)(a),
  subparagraph 130(1)(b)(ii) or paragraph 131(1)(b) in respect of a
  proposal for a development referred to in subsection (1), other than a
  development referred to in subsection (3), the Review Board may, with
  the approval of the federal Minister,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} enter into an agreement with the Minister of the
    Environment in accordance with subsection 39(1) of the [Impact
    Assessment Act](/eng/acts/I-2.75) to provide for an examination by a
    review panel, if that Act applies in respect of the development in
    the region or province referred to in subsection (1); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, enter into an agreement with an
    authority responsible for the examination of environmental effects
    of such developments in that region or province to provide for the
    coordination of their respective examinations of the environmental
    impact of the development or to provide for the examination of that
    impact by a joint panel established for that purpose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time limit

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} An agreement made under paragraph (2)(a), or an
  agreement made under paragraph (2)(b) that provides for an examination
  by a joint panel, shall be entered into within three months after the
  day on which the Review Board is advised, as the case may be,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} of an order for an environmental impact review made
    under paragraph 130(1)(a), subparagraph 130(1)(b)(ii) or paragraph
    131(1)(b); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with subsection 130(4.07), that the
    proposal has not been referred to the Minister of the Environment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by federal
  Minister

  [(2.2)]{.lawlabel} The federal Minister may, at the request of the
  Review Board, extend the time limit referred to in subsection (2.1) by
  a maximum of two months to take into account circumstances that are
  specific to the proposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by Governor in
  Council

  [(2.3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the recommendation
  of the federal Minister, further extend the time limit extended under
  subsection (2.2) any number of times.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Excluded period

  [(2.4)]{.lawlabel} If the Review Board requires the person or body
  that proposes to carry out the development to provide information, or
  collect information or undertake a study with respect to the
  development, then the period that is taken by that person or body, in
  the Review Board's opinion, to comply with the requirement is not
  included in the calculation of the time limit under subsection (2.1)
  or of its extension.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Environmental impact review

  [(2.5)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsections (2.1) to (2.4), if, within the
  time limit set out in those subsections, an agreement has not been
  entered into under paragraph (2)(b), a panel of the Review Board shall
  conduct an environmental impact review of the development, but the
  review shall be limited to the part of the development to be carried
  out in the Mackenzie Valley.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agreement --- Wekeezhii

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If an environmental impact review is ordered under
  subparagraph 128(1)(b)(i), paragraph 128(1)(c) or 130(1)(a),
  subparagraph 130(1)(b)(ii) or paragraph 131(1)(b) in respect of a
  proposal for a development that, as determined by the Review Board, is
  to be carried out partly outside the Mackenzie Valley and either is to
  be carried out partly in Wekeezhii or might have an impact on the
  environment in Wekeezhii, the Review Board shall enter into an
  agreement for the purpose of jointly establishing a review panel and
  prescribing the manner of its examination of the impact on the
  environment of the development

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} with an authority responsible for the examination
    of environmental effects of the part of the development that is to
    be carried out outside the Mackenzie Valley; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} with the Minister of the Environment if that
    Minister is authorized under subsection 39(1) of the [Impact
    Assessment Act](/eng/acts/I-2.75) to enter into such an agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where no agreement

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (3), if, within the period fixed
  by the regulations, an agreement has not been entered into under that
  subsection, a panel of the Review Board shall conduct an environmental
  impact review of the development, but the review shall be limited to
  the part of the development to be carried out in the Mackenzie Valley.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report --- review panel or joint panel

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Within 15 months after the day on which a review
  panel or joint panel is established by an agreement referred to in
  subsection (2) or (3), the panel shall make a report of its
  examination to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the federal Minister, who shall distribute it to
    every responsible minister;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any designated regulatory agency from which a
    licence, permit or other authorization is required for the carrying
    out of the development;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a joint panel referred to in
    paragraph (2)(b) or (3)(a), the minister of the federal, provincial
    or territorial government having jurisdiction in relation to
    examinations conducted by the authority referred to in that
    paragraph; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the Tlicho Government, if the development is to be
    carried out partly on Tlicho lands.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by federal
  Minister

  [(5.1)]{.lawlabel} The federal Minister may, at the request of the
  review panel or joint panel, extend the time limit referred to in
  subsection (5) by a maximum of two months to take into account
  circumstances that are specific to the proposal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time limit by Governor in
  Council

  [(5.2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the recommendation
  of the federal Minister, further extend the time limit extended under
  subsection (5.1) any number of times.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Excluded period

  [(5.3)]{.lawlabel} If the review panel or joint panel requires the
  person or body that proposes to carry out the development to provide
  information, or collect information or undertake a study with respect
  to the development, then the period that is taken by that person or
  body, in the panel's opinion, to comply with the requirement is not
  included in the calculation of the time limit under subsection (5) or
  of its extension.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provisions applicable

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} An examination by a review panel or joint panel
  referred to in subsection (2) or (3) stands in lieu of an
  environmental impact review of the proposal referred to in that
  subsection and paragraphs 134(1)(b), (d) and (e) and sections 135 to
  137.2 apply, with such modifications as may be required, in respect of
  the examination, except that a recommendation of a panel may not be
  referred back to the panel for further consideration.
